## H O M I L Y  X I.

<margin>Divine fire.</margin>
<image-desc>An ornamental woodcut drop cap 'D' decorated with floral scrollwork.</image-desc>THAT heavenly and divine fire / which Christian people inwardly grasp in their hearts / now in this present life: that same fire which now dwells in their hearts / shall outwardly appear / and shall join the members together again / after the body shall have been undone and dissolved: and shall bring about the resurrection of the dissolved members. For just as the fire that served the altar at Jerusalem / was buried in the pit at the time of the captivity <note>original: "ghedoluen was in de gracht"; this refers to a story in 2 Maccabees 1, where the sacred altar fire was hidden in a dry cistern during the Babylonian exile and later recovered as a thick liquid that burst into flame</note>: and the same fire was as if renewed / and served again according to custom / after the temple was built / and the captives had returned: So also this body / which is now common to us (which is turned into mud when it is dissolved) is remade and renewed by the heavenly fire / which awakens our corrupted bodies. For the fire that is now hidden within the heart / shall then be made manifest / and awakens the bodies. In the time of Nebuchadnezzar, the fire burning in the furnace was not a divine fire / but it was a creature <term>creature: here meaning a natural, created element of the physical world rather than a supernatural force</term>: And the three children <note>A reference to Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego from the Book of Daniel, who were thrown into a fiery furnace but remained unharmed</note> who were righteous / when they lay in that visible fire / had in their hearts the divine and heavenly fire / serving in their thoughts and working within them: Which fire came forth from them / and went between them / and prevented the outward fire / so that it should in no way burn or hurt the righteous.

Even so, the soul, being drowned in the abyss of darkness and in the depth of death, is suffocated and dead to God’s face, in the midst of loathsome beasts <note>a reference to demonic forces or terrifying spiritual entities</note>. 

And who can descend into those hidden places and depths of hell and of death, except the skilled craftsman who created the body? Certainly, he travels deep into two parts: namely, into the depths of hell, and into the innermost ground of the heart, where the soul with its thoughts is held fast by death; and he brings the dead Adam <note>Adam here serves as a universal symbol for the fallen human soul</note> out of the dark depth. But he who has no foundation does not fear God the Lord so much, nor is his heart anxious, and he is unafraid: nor does he watch over his heart and his members well to ensure they do not walk in a disorderly manner: such a person has already lost his soul, for he has not yet entered into the struggle.

One person is he who is in the struggle and distress, and another is he who does not know what war <note>original: "oorloghe," used here to describe the internal spiritual battle between good and evil</note> is. ...after the image and after the likeness of God: on the other hand, because he has lost that image through which he had received the promise of the entire heavenly inheritance. Just as if a coin, bearing the King's image, were defaced <note>original: "vervalscht," meaning corrupted or counterfeited</note>, not only does the gold itself suffer, but the image is also of no more value: Such has also happened to Adam, for whom great riches and a very fine inheritance were prepared, like a very large estate <note>original: "hoeve," a farm or manor</note> with much income, wherein stand fruitful vineyards, in some places well-tended fields, yonder herds of cattle, elsewhere gold and silver: Thus Adam's vessel <note>The human person viewed as a container for God's spirit</note>, before his disobedience, was like a precious place: but after evil thoughts and contrived schemes <note>original: "versierde vonden," clever but deceptive inventions of the mind</note> were contained within it, he perished from God’s presence. Yet we do not say that he has entirely perished, been annihilated, or died; but that he has died regarding God, yet his nature lives. For see, the whole world walks and trades upon the earth: but the eye of God sees the heart and the thoughts; it looks all around and from above downward as if with disdain <note>original: "veronweerdigende," regarding something as unworthy or base</note>, and makes no fellowship with them: for they do not think of what is pleasing to God. Just as if there were somewhere low taverns <note>original: "pis-taueernen," literally 'piss-taverns,' referring to squalid or disreputable alehouses</note> and brothels or similar places where many disorders and improprieties <note>original: "onghetijdicheden," meaning unseemly or untimely acts</note> are commonly committed: the godly person passing by loathes and finds this abominable, and seeing, they do not see, as if it were all dead to them: Even so God looks around at those who turn away from Him and from His word: but He despises them, nor has He any fellowship with them: nor does the Lord rest in their thoughts.

### Homily XII.

[be]headed, others plucked <note>Likely referring to the tearing of hair or limbs</note>, and others tortured in various ways. Yes, even the Lord of the Prophets and Apostles, as if forgetting his divine glory—how did he walk? Setting himself as an example for us, he was despised, wore a crown of thorns upon his head, and suffered spitting, strikes to the cheek, and the gallows of the cross. For this reason, because God walked thus upon the earth, so you must follow him. Seeing also that the Apostles and Prophets lived in this way, we must be their followers, if we wish to be built and established upon the foundation of the Lord and of the Apostles.

<margin>1 Corinthians 11:1</margin>
For the Apostle says through the Spirit: Be followers of me, even as I am a follower of Christ. If you embrace the glory of men, and desire to be honored by them, and seek to live in pleasure, then you have turned away from the path. For you must be crucified with the Crucified One, and must suffer with the one who suffered, so that you may also be glorified with the Glorified One. For the bride must suffer with the bridegroom, and thus become a companion and co-heir of Christ. For it is granted to no one—without tribulations, or by any other way than through the sharp and narrow path, full of hardships—to come into the city of the saints, and to rest and reign with the King in unending eternity.

# Of Adam's state after the transgression.

Let us exert ourselves like good merchants, that we might obtain the heavenly inheritance and that which is for the salvation of our souls. Let us learn to obtain those inheritances and possessions that shall remain with us. For if man begins to fathom the mind of God and to say: "I have found and understood something," it shall be found that human understanding exceeds its limits <note>The original Dutch *te buyten gaet* suggests overstepping one's proper bounds.</note> in trying to grasp the mind of God. But in this, he errs greatly. For the more you desire to search out and fathom with the reason of knowledge, the more you shall sink into the depths, and you shall not comprehend. For that which you ponder—what happens to you daily, and how—these are hidden and incomprehensible things, which we must only receive and believe with thanksgiving. 

For have you, from the time you were born until this present hour, been able to know your own soul? Tell me now the thoughts that swarm within you from morning until evening. Tell me the fashioned <term>versierde: the constructed or "decorated" thoughts produced by the imagination</term> thoughts of three days ago. You cannot do this. If you then have not been able to track the thoughts of your soul: how can you examine the thoughts and the understanding of God? Rather, eat as much bread as you shall find and leave the rest of the earth [be]; go hence to the bank of the water-stream and drink as much as you need, yet do not examine too carefully from whence it has its origin, or how it flows. 

Only take care that your foot be healed, or the defect of your eyes, so that you may see the light of the sun; yet do not examine or ask how much light the sun has, or in what sign <note>A reference to the signs of the zodiac, which were used to track the sun's position throughout the year.</note> it rises. That which comes to your profit, take that. For why do you go to the mountains and ask how many wild asses or beasts go there to graze? A little child, when it comes to its mother's breasts, consumes the milk and is nourished; yet it cannot examine the root or the <note>original: "fonteyne" (source/fountain)</note>...

# Homily XII.

G ii

...fountain from which the milk flows out in this way. The infant sucks that milk and drinks the whole measure of milk, and the breast is filled again at another hour: the little child does not know this, nor does its mother: even though in truth the milk comes forth from all the motherly limbs. If you then seek the Lord in the deep, there you shall find him performing signs. If you seek him in the pit <note>This refers to the biblical story of Daniel in the lions' den.</note>, there you shall find him in the midst of two lions, protecting the righteous Daniel. If you seek him in the fire, there you shall find him helping his servants. If you also seek him on the mountain, there you shall find him with Elijah and with Moses. So he is everywhere: both under the earth, and above the heavens, or with us; he is everywhere. So too is the soul with you, in you, and outside you. For wherever you wish, there is your thought—in very distant lands, be it westward or eastward: or even in heaven, there it is found. Let us then labor above all else to have within ourselves the brand-mark <term>brandt-teecken: a physical or spiritual mark of ownership, similar to how livestock are branded to identify their master.</term> and the seal of the Lord. For on the day of judgment, when the separation <note>The original Dutch *schepdinghe* refers to the judging or "sorting out" of all created things.</note> shall be done by God, and all the generations of the earth from Adam’s children shall be gathered, as the shepherd shall call his flock: then all those who have the brand-mark shall know their own shepherd. The shepherd shall also know those who shall be marked with his mark, and shall gather them from among all nations. For his own hear his voice and follow him. And the world shall be divided into two parts: the one flock shall be full of darkness, which shall go into the eternal fire: the other shall be full of light, and shall be led to the heavenly rest. That same thing which we now possess in the soul shall then shine and be revealed, and glory shall clothe the bodies. The fast of Moses.

There is another food besides this visible kind. For when Moses climbed up the mountain, he fasted for forty days: he had climbed up as a man, and he came down having God. For he was hosted by God, and his body was governed by another heavenly food: For God’s word was to him as food, and the glory shone in his countenance. And that which happened there showed the figure of another thing: for that glory now shines inwardly in the hearts of Christians. Woman praying with an uncovered head.

**Question.** What is that: a woman praying with an uncovered head? **Answer.** Since in the time of the Apostles they let the hair grow long as a covering or a hanging veil, therefore the Lord came with the Apostles to correct the creature. The woman bears the prototype <term>voor-forme: a symbolic representation or early model of a spiritual reality to come.</term> of the church. And just as at that time women wore their hair hanging long externally in place of a head-covering: so the church clothes and adorns her children with divine and glorious garments. But in the ancient times, there was a gathering of the Israelite churches, which was protected by the Holy Spirit: and the children of Israel had put on the Spirit for a glory, even if they did not always perfectly harmonize with it. <margin>Luke 10:40</margin>
**Answer.** The Lord, taking up beforehand what Mary should have said to Martha, tells her that Mary had forsaken all things and sat at the Lord’s feet, and praised and thanked God the whole day through. You see here that the benefit of sitting with Him is granted because of love. But hear further, so that the divine word may shine more clearly. If someone loves Jesus and pays heed to Him—if (I say) they diligently and not weakly pay heed to Him, but remain steadfast in love—then God intends to give such souls something in return for such love: even if the person does not know what they shall receive, or what kind of portion God shall give to the soul. For He did not merely <note>original: *slechtelick*; meaning simply or solely.</note> stay with Mary, who loved Him dearly and sat at His feet, but He gave her a secret power from His own substance. For the words that God spoke quietly to Mary were spirit and power; such words went deep into the heart—the soul into the soul, and the spirit into the spirit—and the divine power grew in her heart. For that power necessarily remains firmly in the one into whom it is poured, just like a fixed possession or inheritance <note>original: *erue*; a legacy or landed estate that cannot be alienated from the owner.</note> which cannot be taken away. Therefore the Lord, well knowing what He had given to Mary, said: *Mary has chosen the very best part.* But shortly thereafter, that which Martha willingly...

->What fruit God demands from a Christian person.<-

## H O M I L I E  X I I I .

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'A' at the beginning of the homily, featuring ornate foliate scrollwork.</image-desc>
ALL that is seen, God has created and has given to mankind so that they might delight and rejoice therein. He also gave them the law of righteousness. But from the coming of Christ onward, He seeks another fruit and another righteousness—namely, purity of heart, a good conscience, beneficial <note>original: "oorborlicke", meaning profitable or useful for the soul</note> words, pure and upright thoughts, and furthermore all the services and duties <term>ampten: spiritual offices or roles</term> of the saints. For the
<margin>Matthew 5:20</margin>
Lord says: Unless your righteousness be more abundant than that of the Scribes and Pharisees <note>The religious authorities of Jesus's time, often portrayed as focusing on external rules rather than internal transformation</note>, you shall not be able to enter the kingdom of heaven. In the law it is written: You shall not commit adultery. But I say to you: Do not be covetous, nor be angry <note>original: "grain", an archaic term for wrathful or bitter</note>. For he who wishes to be God's friend must guard himself from the filthiness of sin and from the eternal fire which grows within us. This shall make us worthy of the Kingdom. Glory of the grace and the kindness of the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit. **A M E N.**

### Those who dedicate and surrender themselves to God do not do so without hope of recompense.

->HOMILY XIV.<-

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'A' featuring floral motifs and a figure.</image-desc>

All works that are seen in the world are performed upon some hope: namely, to receive the reward of the labor. For this reason, unless one firmly believes that they shall enjoy the fruits of the labor, they do not advance at all. For the farmer <term>landt-man: literally a 'land-man' or husbandman</term> sows in the hope of receiving fruit, and he endures the bitterness of the labor because of that expectation. Saint Paul says, <margin>1 Corinthians 9:10</margin> The one who plows must plow in hope. And the one who marries a wife usually does so in the hope of having heirs. The merchant also, for the sake of profit, sets himself to the sea and risks his life. Likewise, in the kingdom of heaven, through the hope that one has received to have the eyes of the heart enlightened, he gives himself over, abandoning worldly things, and devotes himself to fervent prayers, waiting for the Lord—waiting for when He shall come and manifest Himself to him, and cleanse him from the sin that dwells within him. Nevertheless, he does not trust in his works, nor in the evidence of his life, until the time that he obtains that which he is hoping for, and until the Lord comes to dwell in him with all the mind and power of the Holy Spirit. But when he tastes the sweetness of the Lord, and when he is gladdened by the fruits of the Spirit, and when the veil <note>original: "decksel", a covering or lid; here referring to the spiritual blindness or 'veil' mentioned in 2 Corinthians 3:14</note> of darkness is taken away—so that then the light of Christ begins to shine and to show its power with an unspeakable joy—then he is strengthened, having the Lord with him through great love: Just as

<summary>This page introduces Homily XIV, drawing parallels between worldly labor—like farming, marriage, and trade—and the spiritual life, arguing that both are sustained by the hope of a future reward. It emphasizes that true spiritual progress is marked by the indwelling of the Holy Spirit and the internal experience of Christ's light.</summary>

# Homily XIV.

the merchant rejoices when he makes a profit, but is nonetheless anxious and afraid of murderers and the spirits of wickedness, lest through negligence he lose his labor anywhere: until he comes into the kingdom of heaven, into the city of Jerusalem which is above. For this reason, let us also pray to God from the heart, that he may cause us to put off the **old man** <term>old man: a biblical term (from the Greek 'palaios anthropos') referring to the sinful, unregenerate nature of a person before spiritual rebirth</term>, and that he may cause us to put on the **heavenly Christ** from this time forth, so that we, rejoicing and being guided by him, may live in all tranquility. For the Lord said, when he wished to give us an abundant taste of the kingdom, **Without me you can do nothing.** But through the <margin>John 15:5</margin> service of the Apostles he can also enlighten many people (for those who were otherwise creatures nourished their fellow-brothers) and can make a brother or a son of Christ, and something more excellent than other humans: for these made holy the heart and the understanding, likewise the thoughts, in order to behold God with steadfast eyes: who secretly gave life, and was helpful to the heart, commending himself to it. For when someone dedicates and surrenders his secrets—namely, the understanding and the thoughts—to God, not busying himself with any other matter or idle thought, but constraining himself with force: then the Lord makes this person a partaker of the mysteries and secrets in greater holiness and purity: and gives himself as a heavenly food and as a spiritual drink. Just as if someone who has much property, many servants and sons, fed his servants with one kind of food, and his own sons born of his seed with another <note>The original Dutch repeats "andere spijse" (other food) for both, but the context implies a distinction in quality or status between the servants' rations and the sons' inheritance.</note>: for the sons are the father's heirs, and they eat with him as being almost equal to their father: Even so Christ, who is the True Lord, has created all things, and feeds the evil and the ungrate- <meta>catchword: ungrate-</meta>

For this reason, if we wish to be born of the heavenly father, we must do something more excellent and better than other men; namely, we must live with diligence, labor, zeal, love, and good conduct in life, in faith and in fear, as those who desire to obtain so great a good and to possess God with <margin>Psalm 16:5</margin> a hereditary right. For one creature nourishes and makes alive another creature, just as the seeds of corn and barley are nourished by the clouds, which are nevertheless themselves creatures. In the same way, the rain and the sun also make living that which is commanded to them. ### Homily XIV. 97

...dwell in. There is also an "earth" in the air where the birds fly and live; but if they choose to stay or walk upon the ground, they are caught by the fowlers. There is also an "earth" for the fishes, namely the water of the sea. Thus, each lives, feeds, and rests in the place where it was born, whether on the earth or in the air. <margin>The earth and land of the devil.</margin> In this same way, there is also Satan's earth and land, where the powers of darkness and the spirits of wickedness dwell, walk, and rest. 

And there is also a light, clear, and divine earth, where the orders of angels and holy spirits, placed between the two, walk and rest. Thus, neither can the dark earth be seen or felt with these physical eyes, nor is the divine and light earth felt or seen with fleshly eyes; but it is revealed to the spiritual—namely, to the eyes of the heart. So it is that the earth which belongs to Satan is seen with the eye of a heart of darkness, and that which is light is seen with the divine eye of the heart. 

Just as is told in worldly accounts—<margin>fiery mountains.</margin>—one finds fiery mountains where the fire remains, and there are beasts there like sheep. To catch these, the hunters make iron wheels <note>original: "raeders"; likely referring to iron traps or circular grids used in the intense heat</note> and cast long hooks <note>original: "angelen"; fishing hooks or gaffs</note> into the fire, because fire is to these animals in place of fodder, drink, rest, forest, life, and all things. If you bring these animals into another air, they die immediately. When the coverings <note>original: "cleedinghen"; the pelts or skins of the animals</note> of these same animals are stained with filth, they are washed not with water, but with fire, and they become purer and whiter because of it. 

In the same way, Christian people have this heavenly fire in place of food, which is a rest for them: it purifies, washes, and sanctifies their hearts; it gives them growth, and it is their light and life. If they depart from it, they are killed by the evil spirits, just as the animals die that [depart] from...

### How the Christian soul must conduct herself.

<margin>1 Cor. 9:27</margin>

It is arranged in the same way in spiritual matters. For it is quite likely that someone has three members of the spirit healthy, but he is not for that reason perfect. <meta>This page continues the theological discourse on the nature of the Christian soul, specifically moving into a discussion on the physical and spiritual reality of the Resurrection. It addresses the skepticism regarding how the human body can be restored and transformed while maintaining individual identity.</meta>

### How the Christian soul must bear itself.

<margin>Concerning the resurrection.</margin>
[at the] resurrection, will all members rise? **Answer.** All things are very possible for God. He has promised that it would so happen: although to human thought it seems to be as if impossible. For just as God, taking from the dust and from the earth, has created another nature—namely the nature of the body, which is unlike the earth—and has also created many kinds, such as hair, skin, bones, and sinews. And just as a needle cast into the fire, changing its color, is turned into fire, yet the nature of the iron is not entirely destroyed but remains: so also in the resurrection, all members rise, nor does there
<margin>Luke 12:7</margin>
perish a single hair, as it stands written: all the members become clear and bright, and are bathed <note>original: "gebetwet"; literally "wetted" or "moistened," here used metaphorically to mean soaked or permeated with light.</note> with light and with fire. They are changed, but they are not (as some say) entirely undone or changed into fire in such a way that their nature no longer remains standing. For Peter is Peter, Paul is Paul, Philip remains Philip; each in his own nature and state, filled with the Spirit. If you say firmly that the nature is dissolved or undone, then there is now neither Peter nor Paul, but God is wholly and everywhere; neither do those who go into pain feel the punishment, nor do those who come into the kingdom of heaven perceive the benefit. Just as if (so to speak) an orchard planted with various fruit trees—pear trees, apple trees, grapevines—with fruits and with leaves, were entirely changed, and that likewise all the trees and leaves were turned into another nature, and that they all became light and clear: so people are also changed in the resurrection, and their members become holy and translucent <note>original: "doorluchtich"; can mean illustrious, but in this context of light, it refers to a body that allows light to pass through it or radiates light.</note>. Therefore, godly people must prepare themselves for the combat <note>original: "camp"; refers to a field of battle or a contest.</note> and battle. And just as a brave youth endures the blows and the struggle, and

# Homily 15.

<margin>The way of the Christian religion.</margin>
strikes back: In the same way, Christian people must also endure outward tribulations <note>historical term for great distress or suffering</note> and inward wars, so that, being tormented, they may overcome through patience. For this is the way of the Christian religion: that wherever the Holy Spirit is, immediately thereafter comes struggle and persecution. You see how the Prophets suffered persecution from the people of their own kin, in whom nevertheless the Spirit was working. You see how the Lord, who is the way and the truth, was persecuted by no other people but His own; namely, by the very race of Israel, by whom He was crucified. Likewise also the Apostles. Thus, immediately after the cross, the Holy Spirit came over from there and went to dwell with the Christians: so that henceforth no Jew suffered persecution <note>The author likely refers to the shift in biblical narrative where the focus of divine "dwelling" and subsequent worldly opposition moves from the Jewish prophets to the early Christian community.</note>, but the Christians have endured martyrdom. Therefore, they must not be astonished: For it is necessary that the truth suffers persecution.

**Question.**

Some say that vice comes in from the outside: and if the person so wills, he does not receive it, but drives it away. <margin>The power and might of sin.</margin> **Answer.** Just as the serpent, having spoken with Eve, was born within her—because she was obedient to the serpent—so also now: because man is obedient, therefore sin enters within, which was outside. For sin has the power and the freedom to enter into the heart; seeing that thoughts do not come from the outside, but inwardly out of the heart. For the Apostle says: I desire that men should <margin>1 Tim. 2:8 <note>Original text cites "1. Tit. 2. e.", likely a scribal error for 1 Timothy 2:8.</note></margin> pray without wrath and evil thoughts. For <margin>Matt. 15:19</margin> according to the Gospel, thoughts come out of the heart. Go then to prayer, and examine your heart and your thoughts, and desire to pour out a pure prayer before God; and look well whether there is any hindrance: namely, whether the prayer is done purely, or whether your soul is preoccupied with the

### How the Christian soul must conduct itself.

Lord, just as the farmer’s heart is with the cultivation of the land, the man’s heart with his wife, and the merchant’s heart with his trade. Furthermore, consider whether you bow your knees in prayer and whether others do not draw away your thoughts. But you say that the Lord, by His coming, has condemned sin through the cross, and that it no longer rests within. Truly, just as a horseman who has placed his wagon in a house has the power to go in and out of that house as he pleases: so also can sin lead thoughts into the heart. For it is written <margin>Luke 22:3 <note>original: "Luc. 22. a." The letter 'a' refers to the first section of the chapter in early modern Bibles.</note></margin> that Satan entered into Judas's heart. If you further say that sin is condemned through the coming of Christ, and that after baptism wickedness has no place in the heart to be nourished there and to hold counsel <note>meaning to entertain or deliberate on sinful ideas</note>. Do you not know how many have been baptized from the coming of the Lord until this time, who nevertheless occasionally thought evil things? Have there not been some of that number who turned to vain glory, to fornication, and gluttony? Are all worldly people who frequent the church pure and blameless of heart? Do we not find that after baptism many sins are committed, and that many people do wrong? Thus the robber <note>a metaphor for sin or the devil as a thief of the soul</note> still has nourishment after baptism, and he may enter and do as he pleases. It is written: <margin>Deuteronomy 6:5 <note>original: "Deut. 6. a"</note></margin> You shall love your Lord your God with all your heart. But you say: I love Him, and I have the Holy Spirit. Do you possess such remembrance, love, and burning desire for God that you are bound fast to it night and day? If you are endowed with such love, truly then you are pure. If you do not have it, then continue to search everywhere: whether earthly dealings, or ugly and evil thoughts have come before you, whether you have not strayed toward them: but rather that your soul is led through con-

### Homily XV.

...with constant love and desire for God. For the thoughts of this world pull the heart in various directions toward earthly and perishable things; they do not allow one to love God or to have remembrance of the Lord. A layperson <term>layperson: a common believer who is not a member of the clergy or a monk</term> often settles himself to prayer on bended knees, and his heart enters into delight, and penetrates the depths so greatly that the opposing path of sins is broken, and he travels deep into vision and wisdom. The powerful, the wise, and the eloquent do not reach this point, nor can they overtake or know the subtleness of his heart, because he deals with the divine mysteries. For he who does not know how to taste precious pearls truly cannot value or appreciate them, because he has no understanding of them. For this reason, Christian people despise and hold as dung <note>original: "dreck"; a reference to Philippians 3:8, where worldly gains are considered "rubbish" or "dung" compared to Christ</note> the precious things that are on the earth, by comparing them with the excellence that works within them.

**Question.** Can a person fall who has the gift of grace? 
**Answer.** Truly he falls, if he is negligent. For the enemies never rest, nor do they hesitate to propose war. Therefore, you must not stop seeking God for a moment. For no small harm is approaching you if you neglect it, even if it seems that you are tested in the mystery of grace. 

**Question.** Does grace remain after the fall of man? 
**Answer.** God desires to bring the man back to life, and He admonishes him again to repentance and sorrow. If he is persistent in sorrow and repentance, He admonishes the man again to have sorrow for what he formerly committed. 

**Question.** Does trouble or war approach those who are perfect, or are they entirely at rest and without care? 
**Answer.** The enemy does not cease to fight against man. Satan knows no mercy, and he persecutes man with

### How the Christian soul should conduct itself.

...with very great hatred: therefore he <note>Referring to Satan, the adversary mentioned in the previous section.</note> does not hesitate to wage war against all people: but he does not attack them all in the same manner. Just as Princes and Counts give tributes to the King; but the King, trusting in his riches, in his gold and silver, gathers those tributes as if from a surplus, not noticing any loss. And just as the one who gives alms does not feel a sense of loss: so also Satan regards these things as overflowing. There is a poor person who lacks daily food, who is plagued and tormented, and yet he cannot complete the work. There is also one who is heavily oppressed with anxiety and pain, and yet he does not grow strong. There is still another, who for the sake of a single word is beheaded <term>beheaded: the Dutch word "onthalt" refers to execution by decapitation, often implying martyrdom in this context</term>, and he grows strong. So too are there among the Christians those who are greatly besieged and stung by sin, who sometimes become stronger and wiser for war, despising the power of the enemies: nor do they fall into any danger because of this: for they are steadfast and at peace regarding their salvation, owing to the constant exercise of war against sins, through which they have become more cautious and more understanding: and thus having God with them, they are guided, and find rest. But the others who are not yet practiced, if any need comes upon them or war is proposed, they immediately fall into death and destruction. Just like those who travel to a city to visit their friends and kin: even if these people meet many people at the annual fair <term>annual fair: "iaer-merct" refers to a large seasonal market or festival, used here as a metaphor for the distractions of the world</term>, they are nevertheless not hindered by them in any way: for they are of the intention to find their friends: and when they knock and call from outside the door, their friends joyfully open the door. <page-num>112</page-num>
-># How the Christian soul must conduct itself.<-

Therefore, it is your choice if you wish to perish; if you wish to blaspheme God, to work with poison, and to bring men to their death: no one can resist or forbid you. But if someone wills it themselves, they are obedient to God and follow the way of righteousness and overcome their lusts. For such a heart that resists and struggles [against sin] can, through firm reason, restrain the power of vices and ugly desires. 

For if in a great house—where there are many gold, gilded, and silver vessels, and many garments, and where gold and silver are abundant—the young men and young maidens who dwell in that same place restrain their hearts (even though nature, through the indwelling sin, desires all things), and because they fear their lords or masters in a human manner, they restrain the power of evil desires: how much more, where the fear of God is taking hold <note>original: "groepende"; likely a variant of "grypende" (seizing/taking hold) or "groeiende" (growing)</note>, must the spirit resist and struggle against the sin that is present in its company. For God has commanded that which is situated within your power.

But the nature of <term>irrational animals</term> <note>original: "onredelicke dieren"; creatures lacking the human capacity for reason and moral choice</note> is bound: just as the nature of the serpent is bitter and venomous, therefore all serpents are such. The wolf is predatory: so all wolves are of the same nature. The lamb, because it is simple, is subject to being preyed upon: therefore all little lambs are of this nature. The dove is simple and upright: so then all doves are of this nature. <margin>What the nature of man is like.</margin> But with man it is not so. For one is a seizing wolf, and another is like a lamb subject to being preyed upon: and yet both come from the same human race. There is one who is not content <note>original: "niet en lijdt"; here meaning he does not limit his desires to his own marriage</note> with his wife and commits adultery; there is another who by no means allows such a thought to enter his heart. There is one who steals his neighbor's goods; there is another who, because he loves God, generously gives away his own.

### Homily XV.

I

You see how fickle nature itself is—that it is inclined toward vice, and yet also toward virtue and honor. It is capable of leaning toward either of these qualities, acting according to its own will. Thus, human nature can grasp and contain both good and evil, whether it be divine grace or the opposing power <note>The "opposing power" refers to the influence of the devil or sin.</note>; but it is not subjected to any necessity. For in the beginning, when Adam lived in purity, he had dominion over his own thoughts. But since he transgressed the commandment, evil thoughts lie like very heavy mountains upon his heart, and they have become mixed with his own thoughts as if they were his own—even though none of them truly belong to him, for they are possessed by vices. Therefore, you must seek the lamp <note>The "lamp" likely refers to the Word of God or the Holy Spirit, which illuminates the inner darkness of the heart.</note> so that once it is lit, you may find pure thoughts. For those whom the Lord has brought forth are natural <note>original: "natuerlick"; here implying those who have returned to their true, God-intended nature through grace</note>; having been raised, they have learned to swim in the sea, and they are not frightened when waves and tempests arise.

<margin>
The state of Christian people in this world.
</margin>
Thus are Christian people positioned: like the understanding of a three-year-old child who cannot reach or comprehend the meaning of a very eloquent orator, because of the great span of time and experience between them. In the same way, Christian people perceive the world like young children, beholding the degree of grace. For they are pilgrims in this world, but their city and rest is another. Christians possess the comfort of the Spirit, tears, sorrow, and mourning; yet these tears are a delight to them. They live in fear alongside joy and rejoicing. They are like people who carry their blood in their hands <note>An idiom suggesting a life of extreme vulnerability, humility, or constant readiness for sacrifice.</note>, not trusting in themselves or thinking themselves to be anything; rather, they are entirely disregarded and viewed as outcasts by all people. It is just as if the King [gave] his treasure to be guarded...

[...if one] gave to a poor man: he who has accepted it to keep does not regard it as his own, but acknowledges his poverty everywhere; nor does he dare to squander from another's treasure, always thinking in his heart that it is not only a foreign treasure, but that the King and a very powerful man has given it to me to keep, and shall demand it back from me whenever he pleases.

Those who have obtained the <term>grace of God</term> <note>original: "gratie Godts"; the unmerited favor and spiritual help of God</note> must be of that same mind: namely, that they feel <term>humbly</term> <note>original: "oodmoedichlick"</note> about themselves and acknowledge their <term>beggary</term> <note>original: "bedelrije"; a state of total spiritual dependence on God</note>. For just as the beggar who has received the treasure from the King to keep—if he trusts in another's treasure as if it were his own wealth and his heart is seized by <term>pride</term> <note>original: "houerdije"; haughtiness or arrogance</note>—then the King takes his treasure from him. And he who was guarding the treasure remains as he was before: namely, a beggar.

So it is also with those who have received the grace of God: if they exalt themselves and their hearts are seized by pride, the Lord takes His grace from them, and then they remain the same as they were before they had received the grace from the Lord.

But some do not realize that the grace dwelling within them is secretly withdrawn through sins. It is just as if there were a youth and also a young maiden in a house; if she, enticed by his fair words, allows him to have his way with her, she commits <term>fornication</term> <note>original: "hoererije"</note> and is worthy of being cast out. In the same way, the horrible <term>serpent of sins</term> <note>a reference to the devil or the corrupting nature of sin</note> keeps company with the soul, enticing and calling to her. If she consents, then the <term>incorporeal soul</term> <note>original: "onlijffelicke ziele"; the spiritual part of a human that exists without a physical body</note> has communion with the incorporeal sin of the spirit—that is, spirit has communion with spirit. And whoever consents commits adultery in their heart and receives the thoughts of the most wicked one.

**Question.** How do Christian people become more excellent and outstanding than the first **Adam** / seeing as he was immortal / and incorruptible in soul and in body: yet Christians die and decay?

<margin>The true death.</margin>
**Answer.** The true death lies within the heart: and is hidden there where the inward man has died. <note>The author distinguishes between physical death and "true" spiritual death, which is the separation of the soul from God.</note> If it happens then that someone comes from death into the hidden life / they truly live in eternity / nor do they die. For even though the bodies of such people are dissolved for a short time / nonetheless they are awakened again in glory: for they are sanctified. So then we say that the death of Christians is a sleep. Were man immortal and incorruptible of body / then all the world / seeing this novelty / namely that the bodies of Christians do not decay or perish / would be pushed by necessity / and not by free will / toward that which is good.

**Question.** Is sin gradually diminished and rooted out / and the man thus advanced toward grace: or is sin pulled out immediately / when the man begins to be advanced? **Answer.** Just as in the mother's body an imperfect child does not immediately become a man / but gradually takes on its features and form: nor does it immediately become a perfect man / but becomes large and manly with length of time: And just as the seed of wheat or barley thrown into the earth / does not immediately bring forth a root / but after the winter and the winds have passed / then the ears of corn come forth at the proper time: Just as also he who plants a pepper tree <note>The pepper plant was a common early modern metaphor for a crop that requires significant time and patience before it can be harvested.</note> / does not immediately receive fruit from it: So also in spiritual things / where there is such great wisdom and subtlety / man gradually becomes greater / and becomes a perfect man / in the fullness of age: not / as some say / by a simple "putting on and putting off" <note>The author is refuting the idea that spiritual perfection is an instantaneous change of state, like changing one's clothes, rather than a process of organic growth.</note>.

### Homily 15.

...new world, to the clear-shining city, and to the angelic people<note>original: "enghelsch volck." In this 17th-century context, "enghelsch" refers to angels, not the English nationality.</note>. Just as various fish fall into the net, and those found unsuitable are immediately thrown back into the sea: so the great fishing net of **grace** is spread out over all, and desires to rest: but humans are disobedient, and therefore they are thrown back into the depths of darkness.

Just as washed gold is found under much sand, and the grains are small like millet: so out of many, few good people are found. For those who shine brightly through works serving the Kingdom, and who adorn His word, are very manifest. Also clear enough are those who are seasoned with the heavenly salt, and who speak of the treasures of the spirit. The vessels are manifest, in which God takes great pleasure and gives His grace. There are also others who with great patience acquire the sanctifying grace, and in various ways, as it pleases the Lord.

The one who speaks, then, cannot instruct every heart unless he be guided by the heavenly light and by the heavenly food: for the <term>exercises</term><note>The Dutch "oeffeningen" refers to spiritual disciplines or the active labors of a believer's life.</note> of men are varied; some are devoted to war, and others to rest. Just as when someone desires to rebuild a ruined city, he first completely casts off everything that is falling: and so he begins to dig, and to lay the foundations in the excavated place, and upon that to build the housing; yet the house is not finished immediately. Just as also he who desires to make a garden in waste and stinking places begins first of all to cleanse, to weed, and to make water-ditches: after that he plants, and that which is planted grows, so that thus after a long time the garden brings forth fruit: So are the wills and desires of men...

### H O M I L Y XVI.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'A' featuring ornate foliage and scrollwork.</image-desc>
ALL substances gifted with understanding—namely, angels, souls, and devils—were created by the creator pure and unfallen<note>original: "onghevallicht." This implies a state of being uncorrupted or not yet "fallen" from grace.</note>. But that some of them turned toward evil came from their **free will**; for they turned away from right reason through their own will. Therefore, if we were to say that they were created as such by the creator, we would then be asserting that God is an unjust judge who cast Satan into the fire. But there are some <term>heretics</term><note>The text likely refers to dualist groups like the Manichaeans, who believed matter was an eternal evil force equal to God.</note> who falsely say that matter has no beginning, and that it is the root, and that this root has a power equal to God’s strength. To these, you may rightly pose this question: Which then is the highest power? Without doubt, it must be the power of God. Therefore, that which is beneath cannot be equal in time or in power. But those who say that God is evil do not know what they are talking about. For in God there is no evil, seeing as He is not <term>passible</term><note>original: "lijdtbaer." # On temptations, of which the first sin is the origin.

...use/ when bodily food is abundant/ they are not without care/ but afterward they expect hunger and barrenness: on the other hand/ they do not become very despondent/ when they are oppressed with hunger or barrenness/ for they know the change very well: Thus it is also in spiritual things: When the soul has fallen into various temptations/ she is not dismayed<note>original: "berbaelt," meaning bewildered or lose heart.</note>/ or despondent: for she knows that she is abandoned by permission<note>God’s "permission" or "concession"—allowing the soul to face trials for its own growth.</note>/ so that she might be tested and instructed concerning evil: likewise/ when she enjoys much riches and rest/ she has no [excessive] anxiety: but expects change. ### Homily XVI.

...also to the servants of God, in whom the overflowing grace not only dries out the desires brought forth by the devil, but also the natural ones: as the men of God are now more excellent than the first Adam. <margin>God is in all places.</margin> God is incomprehensible and indescribable, appearing everywhere, on the mountains, in the sea, and beneath the abyss, not by reason of movement <note>original: "verhulsinghe," likely referring to local motion or shifting from one place to another</note>, like the angels descending from heaven to the earth. For He is in heaven, and He is also here. But you will say to me: How can God be present with the pains of hell, or in the darkness, or in Satan, or in other stinking places? I say that He is without any passion <term>passion: "passie," here meaning the capacity to be affected, changed, or harmed by external things</term>, and that He encompasses it all, seeing that He is indescribable: But Satan, because he is His creature, is bound. Goodness is not defiled by any filthiness, nor is it darkened. If you deny that He encompasses all—the abyss, and Satan—then you make Him circumscribed <note>original: "beschrijvelick," meaning limited or bounded by a specific location</note> regarding that place where the evil enemy is: which would provide a reason to seek another being higher than He. For it is necessary that God be the supreme one over all: but through the mystery of the Godhead and His simplicity <term>simplicity: "simpelheydt," the theological concept that God's nature is not composed of different parts and thus cannot be divided or contaminated</term>, the darknesses do not contain Him, even though they are contained by Him: nor can that which is evil partake in the purity that is with God. So then there is no evil resting in God: for He can in no way be harmed or injured. But vice clings to us, for it dwells in the heart; there <margin>Vice is in man.</margin> it exercises its power, instilling dishonorable and impure thoughts, not allowing pure prayers to be poured out, but leading the understanding captive through this world. Thus it has clothed the souls and has also touched the marrow of the bones. Therefore, just as Satan is in the air where God is also present—yet God is in no way moved by emotion <note>original: "affectie," meaning being influenced or stirred by the presence of the other</note>—so also is sin in the soul, where the grace of God is [is/]

### On temptations, of which the first sin is the origin.

### Homily XVI.

<note>original: "Homilie XVI"</note>

### Homily XVII.

L

...Jesus, and without the power of divine strength, <note>The text suggests that without divine power, one cannot truly know Christ or God’s wisdom</note> [no one can] know the mysteries or the wisdom of God, or have been a rich and good Christian. For those who are led and governed according to the **inner man** <term>inner man: a biblical concept referring to the soul or the spiritual self as opposed to the physical body</term> by divine power, they are wise men, pious warriors, and philosophers of God. The Philosophers teach the eloquence of the Greeks; the others are not learned in speaking, but since they are men who honor God, they are glad and rejoiced for the divine grace. Let us then distinguish which are the best.
<margin>1 Corinthians 4:20</margin>
Paulus says, **The kingdom of heaven is in the work and in the power, not in the words.** Someone can easily say that this bread is made from grain; but one must say in what manner it is gradually prepared, kneaded, and baked. To few people is it given to dispute about the vanity of the passions <term>passions: here 'affectien,' referring to worldly desires or emotional impulses that distract from the soul</term> and about perfection. For the Gospel speaks briefly:
<margin>Luke 6:29</margin>
**Do not be angry; Do not covet; If someone strikes you on one cheek, offer him the other also; If someone drags you into court so that he may take your outer garment, give him your under-garment also.** But the Apostle <note>Referring to St. Paul, whose epistles provide detailed instructions on Christian living</note> who followed thereafter, teaches how the work of purity must gradually be fulfilled with patience and long-suffering: first feeding with milk, like young children; then bringing them to growth and to perfection. For the Gospel has said that the garment is made of wool; but the Apostle has explained in detail how it is all put together. Those, then, who use spiritual words without **tasting** <note>original: "smaeckende," implying an experiential "tasting" or internalizing of the truth rather than just intellectual acknowledgement</note> them, are like those who walk through a parched field in very great heat, and being thirsty, paint a fountain from which water flows, and paint how they drink from it—even though their lips and tongue dry up from the great thirst they suffer; or like
<margin>Spiritual words without taste or works.</margin>

<margin>Those who proclaim the words of God must have the Spirit of God.</margin>
So also those who are rich in the Holy Spirit, and who in truth have obtained the heavenly riches, having within them the communication <note>original: "mededeelinghe," referring to the sharing or participation in the Spirit's essence</note> of the Spirit: if they speak the word of truth to someone, or if they share a spiritual discourse with any, intending to refresh <note>original: "vermaecken," which in this context means to provide spiritual comfort, joy, or nourishment to the soul</note> their souls, it happens that they speak from their own riches and the particular treasure which they have and possess within themselves. They refresh the souls of those who hear the spiritual speech, nor do they fear that anything shall be lacking to them; for they possess and have with them the heavenly treasure of goodness, and they draw from it, and refresh those who eat spiritually.

But a poor, deficient man, who lacks the riches of Christ, not having in his soul the spiritual riches which bring forth all goodness of words, of works, of divine thoughts, and of unspeakable mysteries: even if he wishes to speak the word of truth and refresh some hearers, yet not having the word of God within himself according to power and truth—but only repeating, telling, and teaching the words which he has borrowed from various scriptures or heard from spiritual men—this man seems to want to refresh others, and indeed others enjoy his words; but after he has finished, every word goes back again to its own source from whence it was taken. Thereafter, he remains naked and poor once more, having no treasure of the Spirit of his own to draw from, or to help and refresh others; and he himself is not first refreshed, nor does he rejoice in the spirit.

<margin>We must pray with diligence and with faith.</margin>

<margin>How those conduct themselves who possess grace.</margin> For those who have attained <note>original: "verdient," literally "merited," but in this mystical context referring to those who have been granted the privilege</note> to be sons of God, and to be born from above of the Holy Spirit, having Christ shining within them and ruling them, are governed by the Spirit in various ways. Let us bring forth examples of the manifest ways of the world and, in some sense, touch upon the movements of the soul through grace. For those who are led by grace are sometimes situated like those who rejoice at a royal banquet, gladdening themselves with an unspeakable rejoicing and joy. At another time, they are like a bride who, in her marriage, rejoices with her bridegroom through divine delight <note>original: "wellusticheydt," implying a holy, spiritual pleasure or ecstasy</note>. Sometimes they become like incorporeal angels, so light and pure <note>original: "simpel," referring to a single-natured, unburdened spiritual state</note> are they in their bodies.

### Homily XVIII.

->L v<-

<meta>catchword: ringhen</meta>

Sometimes they rejoice as if in a drunkenness of drink, and are drunk in spirit, through the drunkenness of divine and spiritual mysteries <term>mysterien</term> <note>referring to hidden truths of faith revealed by God</note>. At other times they are as if in mourning and lamentation for the human race, praying for the entire race of Adam, and are sorrowful and wailing, burning with spiritual love for the human race. At other times they are ignited by the spirit to such rejoicing and love that they would, if it were possible, take all people into their own hearts <note>original: "inghewandt," literally 'inwards' or 'bowels', used here to mean the deepest seat of compassion</note>, without making a distinction between good and evil. Now and then they humble themselves so far beneath all people, through humility of spirit, that they think themselves to be the very last and the least. Sometimes they are kept by the spirit in an unspeakable joy. At other times they present themselves like a strong warrior <term>criechs-man</term>, who takes up the king's armor and goes forth to battle, fighting valiantly against the enemies and overcoming them: for thus the spiritual person takes up the heavenly weapons of the spirit, and attacking their enemies, fights against them and casts them under their feet. Sometimes the soul rests in very great stillness, clarity, and peace: so that it might wholly enjoy only spiritual delight, unspeakable pleasure, and well-being. Now and then it is instructed through some understanding and hidden wisdom, and through the bottomless knowledge of the spirit, in that which cannot be spoken with tongue or mouth. Sometimes it presents itself like a common person: Thus grace <term>gratie</term> moves diversely therein, and treats the soul in many ways, governing it according to the divine will, and exercising it in various manners, in order to present it pure <term>supuer</term> <note>original spelling of 'zuiver', meaning clean or unmixed</note>, perfect <term>volmaect</term>, and incomprehensible before the heavenly Father. These aforementioned movements of the spirit belong to the highest degree of those who have come nearest to perfection <term>volmaectheydt</term>. For the various govern-

<summary>This page describes the diverse and often contrasting spiritual states experienced by a soul guided by grace, including ecstatic joy, universal compassion, profound humility, and spiritual warfare. These varying experiences are presented as the training necessary to bring the soul to a state of perfection before God.</summary>

# Of the Treasure of Christians.

The workings of the graces of which we have spoken are expressed in various manners, but they work continually in a person, so that one operation immediately follows another.

<margin>
The joy of the godly soul.
</margin>
For when the soul has reached the perfection of the spirit, being entirely purified from all worldly passions <term>affectien</term> <note>original: "affectien," referring to disordered emotional attachments or worldly desires</note> and united with the comforting Spirit through a hidden communion, and mingled with the Spirit, it deserves to become spirit itself. Then it becomes entirely light, entirely eye, entirely spirit, entirely joy, entirely delight, entirely rejoicing, entirely love, entirely compassion <note>original: "inghewandt," literally 'inwards' or 'bowels', used here to mean the deepest seat of mercy and mercy</note>, entirely goodness and kindness. For just as a stone in the middle of the sea is surrounded on all sides by water, so also they, being in all ways mingled with the Holy Spirit, become like Christ, holding within themselves without any change the powers of the spirit’s might, being pure, unspotted, and blameless both within and without. For seeing as they are made perfect through the spirit, how can they bring forth evil fruit outwardly? Rather, the fruits of the spirit shine everywhere and always within them. Let us then also pray to God from the heart, with all faith, hope, and love, that He might give us the heavenly grace of the gifts of the spirit: so that the spirit may instruct and guide us to all divine will, and that He may rule us with His manifold governance. Thus, through His guidance, through the exercise of grace, and through spiritual advancement, we may deserve to come to the perfection of the fullness of Christ, as the Apostle says: "That you might be filled with all the fullness of Christ."

<margin>
Ephesians 3:19
Ephesians 4:13
</margin>
And again: "Until we all come together into a perfect man, to the measure of the stature of the fullness of Christ." The Lord has promised all those who believe in Him and who pray in truth, that He shall give them the mysteries <term>mysterien</term> <note>referring to the hidden or mystical spiritual truths revealed by God</note> of the hidden communion of the spirit.

# The state of Christians is like merchant trade and leavening.

## HOMILY XXIV.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'D' featuring floral and scrollwork patterns.</image-desc>

Christian people are like merchants who make great profit with their trade. For just as these gather earthly profit from the earth, so the others gather from the whole earth the thoughts of their hearts—which had been scattered by the worries of this world—doing this through the means of all virtues and by the power of the Holy Spirit: which is the best and true merchant trade. For this world is set against the world above, and this life is set against the life above. Therefore, it is necessary for a Christian person—who, according to the meaning of the Scriptures, has renounced this world in which he has remained since the time of Adam’s transgression <note>original: "Adams ouertredinghe"; referring to the Fall of Man in the Garden of Eden.</note>, and whose soul has been held fast by allurements—that he be transported in his heart into the other world, and that he dwell with his mind in the high and divine world, as it is written: Our

### Homily XXIV. 173

...all those who are born of Adam, and his whole race, have become partakers of that leaven. Through this, the passions <term>passions</term> <note>original: "affectien"; in this context, it refers to the disordered desires or inclinations of the human heart resulting from the Fall.</note> of sin have so greatly increased and grown in men, that they have come to adultery, to wantonness, to idolatry, to murders, and to other irrational acts, as long as humanity was leavened with sin. This became so powerful among men that they thought there was no God, and they worshipped stones having no life, nor could they comprehend God with their understanding: so deeply had this leaven of evil passions sprinkled the race of the old Adam.

Thus it pleased the Lord, in His coming, to suffer for all men, and to redeem them with His own blood, and to pour the heavenly leaven of goodness into the believing souls who were brought under sin; and thus to fulfill in them—through progress and growth—all the righteousness of the commandments and all virtues, until they are all leavened into goodness as one, and become one spirit with the Lord, according to the word of Paul: so that vice or wickedness would not be able to come to the thoughts of the soul which is entirely leavened with the divine spirit, as it is said: *Love thinks no evil*, and that which 
<margin>1 Corinthians 13:a</margin> 
follows after. 

But it is impossible that the soul should be sprinkled with the goodness of the Lord, and come to life, without the heavenly leaven, which is the power of the divine Spirit. Just as the race of Adam could not be turned to such great vice and wickedness if the leaven of wickedness—which is sin, and a perceptible power of Satan's spirit—had not entered into it. It is just as if someone kneaded flour without adding leaven <term>leaven</term> <note>original: "deesem" or "heuel"; the fermenting agent (yeast) used to make bread rise. The author uses this as a metaphor for how a spiritual force permeates the entire person.</note> or yeast into it; although he seems to work it, to change it, and to handle it, nevertheless the mixture is without yeast, and is unfit

# Comparison of the Christian State

...to eat: but when the leaven is put into it, it draws to itself the whole batch of flour, and makes that whole batch leavened and fermented, just as the Lord compared the kingdom, saying: The kingdom
<margin>Matthew 13:e, Luke 13:d</margin>
of heaven is like leaven, which a woman took and hid in three measures of flour, until the whole was leavened. Just as also if
<margin>The power of salt.</margin>
someone, wishing to preserve meat well, did not sprinkle it with salt—which used to kill the worms and drive away the bad smell—without doubt such meat becomes stinking; it rots and becomes useless for people. Even so, if you consider that all humanity is flesh, or a dough without leaven, and that you understand that the salt and the leaven—namely the divine nature of the Holy Spirit—is from the other world: then the soul will not be rid of the old stench of wickedness, the heaviness, the vanity, and the craftiness of the leaven <note>The author uses "leaven" here to refer to the pervasive influence of sin, contrasting it with the "heavenly leaven" of the Spirit.</note> until the holy and good salt of the Godhead, and the heavenly leaven of the Spirit, which is from that world and from that land, is mixed and put into the humbled nature of man.

For whatever the soul seems to do from itself, to labor and to accomplish, standing only on its own power, thinking that it will be able to make a perfect work from itself without the cooperation of the Holy Spirit: in that she is greatly deluded. For she is not fit for heavenly places, nor does she belong to the kingdom, who by herself and from herself alone, without the Holy Spirit, thinks to achieve perfect purity. For unless a person who is tormented by passions <term>passions</term> <note>original: "affectien"; refers to disordered desires or suffering caused by internal spiritual struggle.</note> goes to God and despises the world, and believes in hope and patience to receive another good distinct from his nature—which is the power of the Holy Spirit—and that the Lord from above impresses the divine life into the soul: then such a one shall in no way receive the

<meta>This page continues the discussion from the previous page regarding the soul's inability to achieve purity through its own strength. It concludes the 24th Homily by contrasting the physical passions with the spiritual virtues and the necessity of the Holy Spirit's intervention.</meta>

### Homily XXIV.

true life, nor shall it sober up from the **drunkenness of matter**, <note>A common metaphor in early Christian mysticism where the physical world is seen as an intoxicant that clouds spiritual perception.</note> nor shall the light of the spirit shine in the darkened soul, nor shall the holy day illuminate within it; nor shall it wake from the very deep sleep of ignorance: so that it may, through the power of God and through the strength of grace, truly acknowledge God. If a person does not thus merit receiving grace through faith, they are useless and unfit for the kingdom of God. But on the contrary, the one who has received the grace of the Spirit and has in no way turned away—nor through negligence or transgression treated grace with contempt, and thus struggling over a long period of time does not grieve the Holy Spirit—he may obtain eternal life. 

For just as a person feels the workings of vice within the passions <term>affections: the internal movements of the soul or emotions, often referred to as "passions" in a theological context</term>—namely from anger, desire, envy, sloth, from evil thoughts, and from other irrational passions—so must they feel grace and divine power within the virtues: namely in love, gentleness, goodness, joy, simplicity, and divine rejoicing. This is so that they may become joined with the good and divine nature through the benevolent and holy inspiration of grace. 

But the devotion <note>original: "aendachticheydt"</note> which is tested through progress and growth over the course of time—if it remains always united with grace and is found pleasing—becomes entirely spiritual through this progress. And thereafter, being made holy and pure by the Spirit, the soul becomes worthy of the Kingdom. Glory and worship be to the unspotted Father, and Son, and Holy Spirit, for eternity.

<meta>This page continues the transition from the end of Homily XXIV into the beginning of Homily XXV. The previous page emphasized that spiritual progress requires the soul to be united with grace to become truly spiritual.</meta>

Through disobedience we have been brought into slavery, from which we are delivered through the mystery of the cross. Furthermore, concerning the power of godly tears and divine fire.

### HOMILY XXV.

If the divine law <term>godly law: the spiritual guidance or "Torah" written on the heart rather than on stone</term> is in some people—not written with ink or letters, but planted in hearts of flesh <note>A reference to 2 Corinthians 3:3, where the Apostle Paul speaks of Christ's message being written "not with ink but with the Spirit of the living God."</note>, having the eyes of their understanding enlightened—and being seized with a constant desire that is not sensory or apparent, but invisible and beyond comprehension: these people may, through invincible power, overcome the assaults and offenses of the most wicked one. But those who are not adorned with the word of God, nor instructed in the divine law, being puffed up with pride, think they can in vain renounce the causes of sin through their own will. This [will] is truly condemned through the mystery celebrated on the <margin>The freedom of man.</margin> cross. For the freedom that lies within human power consists in this: that one may resist the devil, but not that one may entirely rule over the passions <term>affectien: internal impulses or emotions often referred to as "passions" in early Christian theology</term>. # On the soul's power, temptation, and redemption.

...our Savior spoke with them after the resurrection. The angels and ministering spirits are also gifted with the clarity of the same light,
<margin>Psalm 103:4; Hebrews 1:7</margin>
as it is written: *Who makes his angels spirits, and his servants a flame of fire.* This same fire, consuming the beam of the inner eyes <note>A reference to the "mote and beam" from Matthew 7:5; here, the "beam" represents the spiritual blindness or sin that obstructs the "eyes of the heart."</note>, makes the soul pure: so that receiving the natural power of sight, it may always behold the wonders of God, according to the intention
<margin>Psalm 118:18</margin>
of him who says: *Open my eyes, and I shall perceive the wonders of your law.* That fire drives away the devils; it takes away sins; it has the power to bring to life; it gives immortality; it enlightens holy souls, and it strengthens the rational powers. Let us then pray to this fire, that it may come to us: so that, always walking in the light, we may never in any way strike our foot against a stone, but shining like lights in the world, hold fast the word of eternal life: so that enjoying divine blessings, we may rest with the Lord in life, glorifying the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, to whom is glory for eternity. AMEN.

**Question.** When the Holy Spirit comes, is the natural desire not rooted out all at once together with sin? **Answer.** I have said before that sin is pulled out all at once, and that man receives the first creation of the pure Adam. ## Homily XXVI. ##

...re <note>The suffix of a word from the previous page, likely "nature" or "heart"</note> keeps its hardness, and yet it is pleasing to God. Another is indeed flexible, straightforward, and upright, who gives themselves over to God: the Lord receives this one too. But He does not consider those spirits pleasing who do not persevere in good works. For Adam’s nature <note>A reference to the "fallen" nature of humanity inherited from the first man, Adam</note> is entirely unstable toward good and toward evil, inclined to accept evil: which it cannot fulfill, even if it wishes. Just like parchment upon which one writes—you may write upon it in various ways as you please, and you may erase it again, for the parchment receives all kinds of writing—so also the hard man has set his will in God; he is converted to the good and is received by God. For God, in order to show the depth of His affections, receives all people and all wills. The Apostles, in whatever cities they arrived, stayed there for a short time: and some sick they healed, and some not.

<margin>Why the Apostles healed some, and some not</margin>

Nevertheless, the Apostles truly desired to raise all their dead to life and to bring the sick to health: but that which they desired did not happen everywhere. For it was not permitted to fulfill everything that pleased them. Even Paul, when he was held by the Governor <note>original: "Ouer-heer"; referring to the official or Ethnarch under King Aretas</note> of the Gentiles—had it pleased the grace with which he was gifted, he would have caused the walls and the Governor too to be broken into pieces; for he had the Holy Spirit, the Comforter <note>original: "vertrooster"; a traditional name for the Holy Spirit, from the Greek *Parakletos*</note>. But he was let down over the wall in a basket <note>Refers to the account in Acts 9:25 where Paul escapes from Damascus</note>. And where was the divine power then, in which he was trusting? These things happened by divine dispensation <note>original: "dispensatie"; the theological concept of God's plan or management of the world</note> and arrangement: so that in some things they would perform signs and miracles, and in others they would not have the power. This was so that the faith of the believers and the unbelievers might be distinguished, and that free will might be shown; and because

### 186 On the soul's power, temptation, and redemption. ###

At the very time that he enjoyed divine grace, and was diligent and fervent in that grace, Satan demanded him and spoke to the Lord: "Because you help him and give him support, he serves you; abandon him and hand him over to me, and he will blaspheme you to your face." Since the soul is delighted with consolation, grace is then withdrawn, and the soul is handed over to temptations. <term>tentacien</term> <note>From the Latin *tentatio*, meaning trials or tests intended to prove the strength of one’s faith.</note> Thus the Devil approaches and puts forth countless evils, such as despair, denial, and evil thoughts: tormenting the soul greatly so that he might darken it and alienate it from the hope it has in God. The soul, being brave and discerning in these plagues and torments, does not become despondent: but what it holds, it holds fast; and enduring all that is done to it through endless temptations, it says: "I shall not depart from Him, even if I should die." If a person suffers and endures until the end, then the Lord begins to dispute with Satan: "You see well how much evil and suffering you have inflicted upon him; nevertheless, he has not been obedient to you, but he serves, fears, and honors me." ### Homily XXXVI. ###

The Devil is put to shame and knows not what to say anymore. For had he known that Job, falling into trials, <note>original: "tentatien"; referring to the biblical tests of faith where God permits Satan to challenge a believer.</note> would have endured them without being overcome, he truly would not have demanded him further, so as to avoid suffering such shame. So it is also now regarding those who suffer afflictions and temptations; Satan is put to shame, and he regrets that he did not accomplish his goal. For the Lord begins to say to him: "See, I have permitted you to tempt him; have you gained the upper hand? Has he been obedient to you?"

**Question.** Does Satan know all the thoughts and counsels of humans?

**Answer.** If one person interacts with another and knows what concerns him—and if you, being twenty years old, know what concerns your neighbor—how then would Satan not know your thoughts, having interacted with you since your birth? For he is now surely six thousand years old. <note>This reflects the traditional biblical chronology common in the 17th century, which estimated the age of the world and the presence of fallen angels from the time of creation.</note> This is not to say that he knows what a person will do before he tempts them. For the tempter or enticer <term>becoorder</term> <note>A Dutch term for one who lures or attracts someone toward sin.</note> tempts and entices, but he does not know whether the person will be obedient to him or not, until the soul surrenders its will to him like a slave. Nor do we say that the Devil knows all the thoughts and perceptions of the heart. For just as a tree has many branches and many limbs, so too there are certain branches of thoughts and counsels which Satan grasps; yet there are also other thoughts and counsels that are not grasped by Satan. For the evil part is more powerful in some things, bringing forth certain thoughts; yet in other things, the human mind overcomes, receiving help and deliverance from God, and sets itself against sin. In some things, it suffers defeat; in others, it has its own way. Sometimes the person goes fervently to God, and Satan knows this and sees that he strives against him; but he cannot the person...

<summary>This page discusses the limitations of Satan’s power, arguing that while he is ancient and observant, he cannot predict human choices or know every thought until a person surrenders their will. It uses the example of Job to show how the Devil is shamed when a believer resists temptation through divine help.</summary>

->On the dignity and the state of the Christian man, and of his free will: with useful questions posed in between.<-

### HOMILY XXVII.

Recognize, O man, your nobility and your dignity, how precious you are, being the brother of Christ, friend of the King, and bride of the heavenly bridegroom. For he who is able to see through the boldness <note>original: *vrijmoedicheyt* — implies a state of spiritual confidence or the soul's natural capacity for freedom.</note> of his soul, can recognize the power and the mysteries of the divinity, and through that become more humble: for man perceives through divine power his own fall. But just as Christ passed through passion and the cross, and is thus endowed with glory, and sits at the right hand of the Father: so you also must suffer with him, and be crucified with him: and thereafter rise again, and sit with him, and be joined to the body of Christ, and reign with him eternally in the other world:

# Homily XXVII.

### N iiij

<margin>Romans 8:17</margin>
If it be that we suffer with him, that we may also be glorified with him.
